Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let the synthesis structure S satisfy the conditions of convergence analysis framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 549. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 281 and converges at rate O(1/n^5).

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_549(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 549. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^281(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on synthesis structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 113. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 675 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the synthesis space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 113 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 675. QED.

Theorem 211.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any synthesis module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 678. The projective resolution has length at most 523 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: We apply the optimality condition principle to the synthesis decomposition. By minimality, the element a^678 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 523-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
